# Engineering Backlog

This is the **single** backlog for cross-cutting or future action items that
emerge from reviews and planning. It is the successor to the old
`deferred-work.md`. Everything that used to land there lands here now.

Routing guidance:

- Use this file for non-urgent optimizations, refactors, or follow-ups that span
  multiple stories/epics, plus review deferrals that are real but intentionally
  not actioned in the PR that found them.
- Must-fix items to ship a story belong in that story's `Tasks / Subtasks`.
- Same-epic improvements may also be captured under the epic Tech Spec
  `Post-Review Follow-ups` section.

How items flow:

1. The BMGD/BMAD review skills (`gds-code-review`, `bmad-code-review`,
   `*-quick-dev`) append each `defer` finding to the **Deferral inbox** below,
   under a dated `### Deferred from:` heading.
2. Triage folds inbox entries into the table as a curated row (and removes the
   raw inbox note once captured). Pick items up when you next touch the area.

   **Standing triage cadence (per-PR, adopted 2026-07-21 per #545 / audit
   AUD-004).** A PR that parks a `defer` finding triages its OWN new inbox
   entries before it merges: fold each into a curated row (with a mirror issue
   when unresolved), mark it absorbed or superseded with a pointer to the
   destination, or retire it with a one-line factual reason. The inbox is a
   hand-off buffer for the change in flight, so it holds only entries from work
   that has not yet merged and does not accrete into a standing archive. The
   weekly-batch alternative
   was the recorded dissent (audit §14a Round 3); per-PR was adopted because the
   inbox had already accreted ~90 dated sections over two weeks under batch-only
   sweeping, the exact invisible-work rot AUD-004 describes: a batch that never
   quite runs leaves findings untracked by both backlogs. The one-time clearing
   pass that adopted this cadence is #545.
3. **GitHub mirror (standing rule, 2026-07-15):** every curated row that is not
   finished has a matching GitHub issue, recorded in the `GH` column as `#NNN`.
   Finished means `done`, `resolved`, `shipped-v1` (its remainders live on
   their own rows), or superseded; every other status is open work and needs a
   live issue. Create the issue when the row lands: matching template title
   prefix (`[Feature]:` / `[Bug]:` / `[Parity]:` / `[Docs]:`; for the internal
   Types, a defect-shaped `review-deferral` or `bug` takes `[Bug]:`, an
   improvement-shaped `review-deferral`, `perf`, `design-decision`, or `task`
   takes `[Feature]:`, a divergence of implemented behavior from 1994 takes
   `[Parity]:`, and docs/process work takes `[Docs]:`), the priority tag
   `[P1]`-`[P3]` in the title (the label taxonomy has no priority axis), and
   the row's notes as the body with a pointer back to the story id. When a row
   finishes, close the issue (completed or not planned) and set the `GH` cell
   to `—`; when an issue is closed on GitHub first, true up the row the same
   way in the next session that touches this file.
   `src/tests/backlogIssueMirror.test.ts` enforces the row half of the
   invariant: well-formed rows, a known status vocabulary, no unresolved row
   without an issue, no finished row holding a live reference, and no two rows
   sharing an issue number.
4. `Type` legend: `review-deferral` (a real finding parked for scope),
   `perf` (a measured/suspected optimization), `feature-request` (unbuilt
   capability awaiting a spec), `bug` (a player-reported defect awaiting a
   fix), `design-decision` (a ruling recorded for the record, often parked
   with its trigger), `task` (a scoped follow-up with a known method).
   `Status`: `open`, `in-progress`, `idea` (feature not yet specced),
   `done` (delivered in full), plus the table's established refinements:
   `partial` (a named remainder is still open), `parked` (deliberately
   shelved with a resurrection condition), `next` (queued), `shipped-v1`
   (first version shipped; the row tracks refinements), `resolved`
   (investigated or fixed and verified; kept for the record, unlike `done`
   it usually closes a defect or a question rather than delivering a
   feature), and `impl-review` (implemented, awaiting review/validation).
5. `Priority` is the do-first order (impact × effort × risk, and whether the
   item is blocked), distinct from `Severity`, which is impact alone. **P1**:
   work next; real correctness/data-safety impact and ready to pick up. **P2**:
   worthwhile, do opportunistically when you next touch the area. **P3**:
   low/cosmetic, blocked on investigation, watch-only, or a feature awaiting a
   spec. The `Priority` value stays strictly `P1`/`P2`/`P3` so the column is
   machine-sortable. Within P3, _ready_ cheap fixes are ordered before _gated_
   ones (blocked on profiling / a spec / watch-only), and each P3 row's Notes
   lead with **Ready.** or **Gated.** so nobody burns a session grabbing a
   blocked "easy P3."
6. Priorities validated by an agent party (game architect + game designer +
   persistence architect, 2026-07-06): P1/P2 and the P3 tier confirmed
   unanimously; the design voice dissented on the VIP-limo row (argued P2 on
   player-visibility), resolved by ranking it first among the P3 cosmetics
   rather than a tier bump.

| 2026-08-09 | refmap-sprites-resampled | #812 | visual-parity | bug | P1 | high | — | open | **Found by the adversarial review on PR #811, no test covers it.** `refMap` (`src/render/sprites/facilities/serviceKit.ts:15-39`) promises "at the canonical bake size the map is the identity, so the port is pixel-exact". Every caller authored its reference at the retired TILE 11 / FLOOR 44, so after the move to 10 x 45 NO caller can hit identity: drawParking 44x44, drawParkingRamp 176x44, drawSecurity 88x44, drawMedical 176x44, drawHousekeeping 88x44, drawRecycling 220x88, against actuals of 40x45, 160x45, 80x45, 160x45, 80x45, 200x90. Every rect now ports through `Math.round(rx * 10/11)`. Measured damage: the security monitor wall (five monitors, reference pitch 10, width 8) lands at columns 5/15/24/33/42, a pitch of 10/9/9/9 with widths 8/7/7/7/7, so a regular 2x5 grid reads visibly irregular; `wallp`'s 12px seam pitch becomes 11/11/10; `box()`'s 1px lit-left and shaded-right edges collapse into one column on roughly one position in eleven; and 45/44 inserts one duplicated pixel row into all six. Fix by re-authoring the six reference maps at the shipped scale, or by deriving the reference size from TILE/FLOOR so identity holds by construction. Art, so it goes through the room-design approval gate in the visual-parity GDD. Also correct `serviceKit.ts:6`, which states the retired footprint as fact. |
| 2026-08-09 | rooms-underdraw-occupants | #813 | visual-parity | bug | P2 | med | — | open | **Found by the adversarial review on PR #811; two assertions are pinned to the reduced behavior with TODO(#813) so it is stated in code rather than hidden.** Furniture pitches inside room layouts are fixed pixel literals while unit widths shrank 9%. Measured max figures drawn at full population, 11x44 vs 10x45: office (pop 6, 9 tiles) 5 -> 4; amusements (pop 25, 12 tiles) 7 -> 6; boutiqueBay (pop 22, 12 tiles) 18 -> 16. The "occupancy is honest, no ghost people" guard only asserts `min(occupants, seats)` for three occupants, so it could not see the seat count fall. Under-drawing is the OPPOSITE of the failure that suite was built for: a worker present but invisible. The same review corrected the suite to use real widths (it asserted against stale 66/121 literals from the retired tile, so it exercised geometry the renderer never produces), and that is what exposed this. A first attempt at deriving the pitches from TILE (`slot = 2 * TILE`, `floor(tw / TILE)`) did NOT restore the counts, so the cause is not only those two literals; diagnose before touching art. Restore the pinned assertions to 3 and 0 with the fix. |
| 2026-08-09 | preview-gallery-dead-scale | #814 | visual-parity | bug | P2 | med | — | open | **Blocks the palette and room-density epics; fix it FIRST or that art is judged against a lie.** `src/preview.ts:45,79-80` renders room art at `TILE = 12` and `h = 44` (44 is exactly the retired FLOOR), so 3.67 tiles per floor against a world of 4.5. `src/gallery.ts:61-63` is independently wrong at tile 18 / floor 52, or 2.9. Both are shipped, screenshotted pages whose job is showing room art at game proportions, and both are what an artist would use to judge the Epic 2 and Epic 3 work in the visual-parity GDD. | 2026-08-09 | session-end-clock-origins | #808 | desktop-analytics | design-decision | P2 | low | — | open | **Deferred from Codex on PR #802 (`src/telemetry.ts:88`), verified before deferring.** `session_end` has three different clock origins and the largest gap is deliberate, so aligning one without the others makes the metric worse. (1) Web starts at boot with nothing awaited before `create()`. (2) Desktop returning + consented starts at boot plus 0 to 3 seconds of save-store hydration, because `startGameplaySession()` sits at `src/bootstrap.ts:153`, ahead of `await prepareSaveStore()` at `:171` and `create()` at `:173`, and the clock has no first-activity gate (`resumedAt` set in `begin()`, `src/analytics.ts:137-139`). (3) Desktop first run does not start at boot at all: `startGameplaySession` early-returns while consent is `pending` (`src/analytics.ts:477`) and `armSessionOnGrant` (`src/ui/uiDesktopAnalytics.ts:71-72`) arms it when the player dismisses the notice, an offset of seconds to minutes that dwarfs case (2). NOT a regression from #802: `src/bootstrap.ts` is not in that diff, the ordering matches `main`, and the gate only made desktop analytics reachable. Codex overstated the magnitude: per-call 3s waits (`BOOT_STORE_TIMEOUT_MS`, `src/game/desktopSaveHydrate.ts:31`) do not compound as implied, since a dead bridge exits early at `desktopSaveStore.ts:103` for 3s flat and a timed-out read aborts hydration at `desktopSaveHydrate.ts:208`; a healthy bridge costs 5 to 50 ms, below the metric's own 1-second rounding. Do NOT take the suggested one-line fix of starting the desktop clock after hydration: it would define desktop as time-after-ready while web stays time-from-boot, two meanings under one name, and it would erase the signal for a player who hits a 3s stall and quits at 4s. Preferred: either record boot/hydration duration as its own property so the pre-play window is subtractable on both editions, or document and align what `session_end` means across all three cases. Settle it once real desktop sessions arrive, since case (3) dominates and cannot be evaluated without first-run data. |
| 2026-08-09 | help-privacy-packaged-edition-tense | #807 | desktop-analytics | task | P2 | med | — | open | **Scheduled revert, not a defect; deferred from the party ruling on the Help privacy copy (PR #802).** `helpPrivacyBody()` in `src/ui/templates/helpContent.ts` ships in the WEB build, so its third paragraph now states the RULE that governs any edition we package instead of describing the desktop app in the present tense. The old wording was false twice over: no desktop artifact existed that a reader could obtain, and the shell canceled the ingest request anyway (`onBeforeRequest` allowed only `app://game`, and the injected CSP was `connect-src 'self'`), so the counts it said were crossing the internet were not being sent. TRIGGER, a distribution event rather than a code event: the first desktop artifact handed to anyone outside this repository, playtest builds included. Merging a stage does not do it. Same trigger as #806. On that trigger, flip "Today Verticopolis runs in your browser, and that is the only edition you can play" and the two "would" sentences back to the present tense, carry the same wording into `_bmad-output/specs/spec-analytics-posthog-migration/transparency-note.md` (Short line and Longer paragraph), update the negative pins in `src/helpPage.test.ts` that currently hold the old sentences out, and reintroduce the CHANGELOG line for the consent flow that was dropped from #802. The paragraph carries TWO edition-conditionals pointing opposite ways, so do not "tidy" either before the trigger: the packaged edition sends gameplay counts a browser does not, AND a browser sends page-visit counts plus Web Vitals that a packaged edition does not (`injectVercelTelemetry` refuses every wrapped build). The first-run notice (`src/ui/templates/desktopAnalytics.ts`) and the Settings Privacy row are deliberately untouched and stay untouched: both render only on a packaged build, where the present tense is already correct. |
| 2026-08-07 | desktop-ingest-web-forgeable-dimensions | #788 | desktop-analytics | review-deferral | P2 | med | — | open | **Deferred from the stage-2 review of the desktop ingest route (#781, Blind Hunter, medium).** `POST /api/ingest/desktop` authors both dimensions server-side, but its sibling `POST /api/ingest` forwards whatever the client body carries, and `originAllowed` passes an absent `Origin`, so `curl -X POST /api/ingest -d '{"event":"boot","properties":{"platform":"desktop","distribution_channel":"steam"}}'` with no `Origin` header lands a fabricated Steam desktop session in the dataset. `analyticsIngestDesktop.test.ts` documents that pass-through today. Until the web route validates the pair too, `platform: "desktop"` is not evidence of where an event came from, and any desktop-versus-web split has to be read with that caveat. Fix shape: on the web route, validate `platform` against `PLATFORM_LABELS` and `distribution_channel` against `DISTRIBUTION_CHANNEL_LABELS`, rewriting anything else to `unknown` the way the desktop route already does. Out of stage 2's scope, which added a route and changed no web-route behavior; the stage-2 comments were corrected to stop claiming the dimensions are server-authored everywhere, and `buildDesktopCaptureBody`'s doc names this issue. |
| 2026-08-07 | ingest-body-cap-chunked-bypass | #789 | desktop-analytics | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | open | **Ready.** Deferred from the stage-2 review of the desktop ingest route (#781, Edge Case Hunter, low, pre-existing). `handleIngestRoute` reads `content-length` and returns 413 only when the parsed number exceeds `MAX_BODY_BYTES`. A request that omits the header (a chunked `Transfer-Encoding` POST) gives `Number(null) === 0`, which is finite and under the cap, so the check passes and `await request.json()` buffers the body with no ceiling of its own. Vercel's platform body limit is the outer backstop, so production is bounded by that rather than by this check. Recorded because the stage-2 module comment newly leaned on the cap as one of the two things that bound abuse; that wording was corrected in the same round to say the cap reads `content-length`, but the gap itself is unfixed and applies to both routes. Fix shape: bound the read itself (stream and count bytes, or take `arrayBuffer()` and reject past `MAX_BODY_BYTES` before parsing) instead of trusting a declared length. |
| 2026-08-07 | desktop-origin-cross-repo-pin | #790 | desktop-analytics | task | P2 | med | — | open | **Deferred from the stage-2 review of the desktop ingest route (#781, Edge Case Hunter, medium).** `DESKTOP_ORIGIN = "app://game"` in `src/analyticsIngest.ts` has to equal the origin the Electron shell serves the game from, and that string lives in the desktop shell repo. Verified correct on 2026-08-07: the shell registers `HOST = "game"` under its custom scheme, so `app://game` is right today. Nothing in either repo fails if the shell changes it. Every desktop event would 403, silently and with no client-side signal, and because the guard also accepts the literal `"null"`, any request whose origin serializes opaquely would keep working and mask the mismatch, so a drift could present as a partial outage rather than a clean one. Stage 5 (the shell allowlist stage) is where the two-repo pin belongs: assert the constant against the shell's host in a check that reads both, or publish the origin from one side and consume it on the other. |
| 2026-08-07 | desktop-ingest-no-cors | #791 | desktop-analytics | design-decision | P2 | — | — | open | **Open question for the client-transport stage; deferred from the stage-2 review of the desktop ingest route (#781, Edge Case Hunter, informational).** `handleIngestRoute` answers any non-POST with 405, and no handler sets `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, so a cross-origin request that would need a preflight fails before it reaches the route and no caller can read the response. The client stage therefore has to post in a form that stays a simple request: `navigator.sendBeacon`, or a `no-cors` `fetch` with a simple content type. The core parses body text and ignores `content-type`, which is what makes both of those work, and the relay's answers carry no body, so nothing is lost by being unable to read them. Also noted in `api/ingest/desktop.ts` so the transport author meets it before writing the client. Decide there whether to keep the endpoint write-only (cheapest, and it is all the relay needs) or to add a preflight branch and an allow-origin echo. Severity is `—` because this records a constraint rather than a defect. |
| 2026-08-07 | desktop-ingest-platform-label-pin | — | desktop-analytics | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | done | **Done 2026-08-07 in the same stage that raised it; close #792.** `buildDesktopCaptureBody` stamps the string literal `"desktop"`, while its sibling `distribution_channel` was already pinned against `DISTRIBUTION_CHANNEL_LABELS` by a test. If the client union ever renamed or re-spelled that label, the desktop route would keep emitting the old spelling, the dimension would split into two values, and nothing would fail. The vocabulary test in `analyticsIngestDesktop.test.ts` now also asserts the stamped literal is a member of `PLATFORM_LABELS`, so the route's hardcoded string cannot drift from the client vocabulary in silence. Mutation-checked red both ways: re-spelling the literal, and dropping `desktop` from `PLATFORM_LABELS`. Recorded as a deferral by the stage-2 review (Blind Hunter, low) and then fixed rather than parked, since a one-line assertion costs less than tracking it. |
| 2026-08-05 | screenshot-27c-nondeterminism | #762 | pixel-art | bug | P2 | med | — | open | **REOPENED 2026-08-05 (recurred on PR #766 after the scrollbar fix merged, so that theory was at best one of two leaks; #762 reopened with the evidence).** The determinism check now saves BOTH legs' copies of every differing file and the workflow uploads them on failure, so the next occurrence names the pixels instead of feeding another inference. Prior note (kept for the record):  The leak was the LAST wall clock in a capture: fluent overlay scrollbars fade on the compositor's own clock, which the stepped TestClock cannot reach, so a shot that scrolls a container (27b/27c scroll the schedule dialog's .modal-box and crop that region) captured whatever fade phase CI load landed on: usually fully faded (the committed gallery shows a clean gutter), occasionally mid-fade, the intermittent failure. The runner now hides scrollbars for every capture (scrollbar-width: none plus the webkit pseudo-element belt), which renders the faded end state every time; overlay scrollbars occupy no layout space in the pinned container, so committed pixels are expected unchanged. Host-vs-CI asymmetry explained the never-reproduces-locally history: Windows classic scrollbars neither overlay nor fade. Original: intermittent 'pixel bytes differ' on 27c-elevator-schedule-unsaved.png, observed twice on PR #759, reruns passed. The pinned-container determinism guard (two independent renders per shard) fails intermittently with pixel bytes differing for `docs/screenshots/27c-elevator-schedule-unsaved.png`, always that one screenshot. Observed twice on PR #759 with clean passes on intermediate pushes of the same branch, so probabilistic, not drift and not diff-related. The scene opens the elevator schedule dialog with unsaved changes; suspects are a time-driven decoration ticking across a frame boundary between the two legs or an animation phase captured mid-transition. Blocks merges at random until fixed (the guard is the hard gate, correctly); reruns pass, which is a workaround not a fix. Fix shape: loop-render the 27c scene in the pinned container and diff, then freeze or advance whatever leaks to a stable frame like the other scenes do. `/bmad-code-review`. |
| 2026-08-05 | desktop-export-single-flight | — | desktop-shell | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | done | **Done 2026-08-06 (#760 closed): `runExportFlow` in `src/game/exportFlow.ts` carries the one-design fix, an owner-token single-flight latch at the sole export choke point plus a 10-minute watchdog that frees the latch with an honest toast and never abandons the awaited call; a run that lost the latch also refuses a late "fallback" (it would open a fresh dialog). Review defers spun out to [[export-fallback-dialog-unlatched]] (#773) and [[export-late-settle-stale-copy]] (#774).** Original note: deferred from the adversarial review of the stored-byte export PR #759 (story D7, D2's AC22). Deferred from the adversarial review (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter, both `defer`) of the stored-byte export PR #759 (story D7, D2's AC22). Two related residuals: (1) no single-flight guard on `onExport` -> `exportGame`; the port contract now requires the shell's dialog to be modal to the game window, so UI reentry is impossible on a conforming shell, but a nonconforming shell or a platform whose app menu is not blocked by a window-modal dialog (macOS) could stack a second flush and dialog. (2) `exportRecord` is awaited raw, deliberately (a save dialog legitimately stays open for minutes, so `withTimeout` is the wrong tool), but a hung bridge then dies silently: no toast, no fallback, and nothing stops repeat fires into the same hung bridge. A naive latch makes (2) worse (a hung bridge would brick Export permanently), so the two want ONE design: a latch released by a watchdog far beyond any plausible dialog session, plus an honest toast. No data-loss branch. The `patch`-grade siblings (malformed resolution read as silent cancel; unstated byte-capture timing) were fixed on the PR. `/gds-code-review`. |
| 2026-08-05 | desktop-icon-1024-master | #761 | desktop-shell | task | P3 | low | — | open | **Gated on art.** From story D7 (desktop packaging, private repo) AC2, party-ratified 2026-08-05. The desktop packager generates platform icons at package time from `pwa-512x512.png`; png2icons soft-upscales an ic10 (1024) icns entry from the 512 source, and the party ruled a blurry 1024 pretending to be real is worse than honestly topping out at 512, so the packager strips ic10. | 2026-08-02 | icon-mapper-leading-marker | #743 | accessibility | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | open | **Ready.** Deferred from the `/bmad-code-review` of the icon PR (#733; Edge Case Hunter + Blind Hunter, minor, latent). `appendMessageWithIcons` / `messageWithIcons` (`src/ui/icons.ts`) match `LEADING_EMOJI_RE = /^(...)/`, so a mapped bulletin emoji becomes an icon only when it is the first character. Safe today: every engine/UI bulletin string leads with its mapped marker at index 0 (exactly one mapped emoji), and `logLine`/`toast` pass `e.text` through with no prefix, so nothing regresses. Latent gap: a future message that prefixes the marker (a leading space, a `"Day 5: 🔥..."` label, a quote) would render the emoji as plain text and tofu again on a no-emoji-font system, the #721 failure, with no error. Fix shape: either a coverage-guard assertion that every emitted bulletin-emoji literal is message-leading, or relax the mapper to skip a leading prefix while still swapping only the first marker. Not fixed in #733 because no current message triggers it and index-0 is the engine's standing convention. `/bmad-code-review`. |
| 2026-08-04 | tdt-legacy-pre-tail-import | #745 | SimTower parity | parity | P3 | low | — | open | **Accepted limitation from PR #742.** A `.TDT` that is ALL of: exported by 2.9.0 or earlier (serviced-floor payload), exported before v1.14.0 (so it stops after the stairs table rather than carrying the 0xFF routing region), stairless, and carrying a skip-floor shaft, has no structure the importer can use to corroborate the legacy layout. Anchoring on the file END was tried and withdrawn one round later: a CURRENT file truncated at exactly the shorter walk end plus finance, parking and stairs is BYTE-IDENTICAL to this shape when both regions are zeros, which they are for a stairless tower with no connected stalls, so an EOF anchor cannot recover the old file without silently mis-reading the damaged one. Silent wrongness is the worse failure, so the old file loses. Both directions are pinned by tests. Player impact: synthesized transports WITH a warning, the pre-#742 behavior for these files. The population is tiny (pre-v1.14.0 exports are the ones that page-faulted the 1994 game on load), and the real fix, if it ever matters, is to stamp our exports with a format generation so the reader has a FACT instead of this inference. |
| 2026-07-31 | tdt-express-desync | #740 | SimTower parity | bug | P1 | high | — | open | **NARROWED 2026-08-05: no global ordering is required, and the loss scales with how EARLY the express sits.** Reordering the game's own 8-shaft fixpoint save, never altering a byte of any record: swapping two STANDARD shafts keeps **8 of 8** (and the game re-saves them in the swapped order, so the table need not be sorted by column, kind, or anything else); moving the express to index 1 keeps **4 of 8**; to index 0 keeps **2 of 8**; left at index 5 keeps all 8. Only the express's position matters, and the kept count grows with its index rather than flipping at a threshold, which argues against a validity check on slot 0 and for something cumulative in the table walk. **Express-LAST is therefore optimal, not merely safe** (it maximizes survivors), though it still cannot save a SECOND express, so the export report's warning stands and this row stays open on the mechanism. **SETTLED 2026-08-05: the trigger is the express record's POSITION in the elevator table, not its length and not its content.** Two probes from the game's own bytes closed it. (1) **Content is identical:** a game-written express record is 51 non-zero header bytes over an ENTIRELY ZERO payload (fixed block, all 8 per-floor entries, car block), and ours is byte-for-byte the same 6,274 bytes plus 26,892 more zeros from span sizing, so splicing the game's record into our file is a literal no-op; the "our zero-fill vs their live state" theory is dead. (2) **Order alone flips it:** reordering the game's own 8-shaft fixpoint save so the express sits FIRST, without altering a byte of any record, drops it to **2 of 8** kept. That explains every earlier row: express last -> everything before it survives; express at slot 5 in the game's own file -> all 8 survive; express first -> express plus one, whichever sizing. **Consequence: express-last is the best available construction of the table, not a workaround**, costing a tower with ONE express nothing. It does NOT solve #740 generally: a tower with a SECOND express still loses it and anything behind it (the 22-of-23 row), the export report still warns about that, and this row stays open. Mechanism still unknown; of the two shapes once plausible, a required record ordering is REFUTED (swapping two standard shafts is harmless and the game keeps the swap), leaving the express being read before structures a later shaft depends on. Earlier rounds (kept for auditability) retired the "8-shaft ceiling" reading via a no-express control that kept 21 of 21, and retired the whole per-floor-count family by showing the game's own record length still loses the shafts behind it. Also pinned: the game WRITES express payloads stop-sized (`194 + 3140 + 324 * inSpanStops + 348`), its re-saves carry stray stop bits outside a shaft's span so readers clamp to the span (ours does, v2.13.0), and the degraded read is a fixpoint. Game-written saves need no `COMMDLG.DLL`: a tower loaded from a path saves back over it via `File > Save`. |
| 2026-07-31 | tdt-1994-room-reachability | #737 | SimTower parity | parity | P2 | med | — | open | **Observed on the Wine harness while validating the spanned-floor elevator payload fix.** With the corrected stride the owner's 4★ `sixseven_14` tower loads and runs in the retail 1994 game (shafts render with their real stop settings, tenants move in, Pop climbs past 640), but the room inspector on an office at floor 63 reads **"No transportation connected"** while our engine reports two standard shafts stopping there (`elevatorStandard@x188`, `@x267`, confirmed by walking the deserialized tower). NOT yet established which side is wrong: it could be a file-fidelity gap (something binding rooms to shafts that we never write, most likely the §11 lobby/reachability table we emit as a blank `0xFF` region, see [[tdt-export-routing-tail]] #310), or a genuine 1994-vs-our-engine reachability difference for a tower designed under our rules (walkable-corridor or two-ride modeling), which would be an engine-parity question rather than a format one. **NARROWED same day (harness):** it is NOT a wholesale table failure. Two minimal towers (one shaft over floors 1-5, one occupied office on floor 3) show the game reading our shafts and grading them normally: an office 10 tiles away inspects with an EMPTY message box, one 200 tiles away reads "Elevator is very far away". Distance alone yields a graded complaint, never "not connected". The sixseven case also survives the stride fix (re-exported at 453,040 bytes, tower runs at 4★ with Pop climbing past 640, floor-63 office still "No transportation connected"), so it is a distinct defect. Next probe: a two-band tower (ground lobby, express stopping only at 1/15/30, a local shaft 30-45, an office at 33) to test whether the game accepts a TRANSFER CHAIN built from our bytes, since floor 63 is reachable only by transferring at the sky lobby on 60. |

| 2026-07-29 | desktop-build-mode | — | mobile-distribution | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | resolved | **RESOLVED in the desktop analytics epic's stage 1 (party ruling 2026-08-07 on #781); issue #710 closes on merge.** The dimension is now two dimensions. `PlatformLabel` is the runtime surface (`web` \| `twa` \| `ios` \| `desktop`), resolved from the BUILD MODE first and the injected `isNativeWrapper` flag only after it, which settles both halves of the gap: a desktop shell that binds a port no longer reports `ios`, and one that binds none no longer reports `web`. A sibling `distribution_channel` prop (`web` \| `twa` \| `ios` \| `steam` \| `itch` \| `unknown`) carries the commercial channel, read on desktop from the port's new optional `distributionChannel` member and accepted only as those two exact literals, so a hostile or mis-stamped shell reports `unknown` rather than minting a value. Both the port member and the emitted property carry the `distribution` prefix because a bare `channel` collides with PostHog's built-in `$channel_type`. Both unions are pinned closed by a runtime list plus a compile-time exhaustiveness check, and `isPlatformPort` deliberately does NOT shape-check `distributionChannel` (a bad value cannot throw at boot, and refusing the port would cost a working shell its native file save). PRD F8 and arch section 9 updated to match. Vocabulary only: the telemetry gate is untouched, so wrapped builds still emit nothing; the consent surface, transport, and server route are later stages of #781. ORIGINAL DEFER (kept for the record): **Gated.** Settle the analytics platform dimension for the desktop shell when desktop analytics is designed (the private distribution repo's plan story 8, `verticopolis-mobile`). `resolvePlatformLabel` (`src/analyticsEnrichment.ts`) maps any port reporting `isNativeWrapper` to `"ios"`, and the desktop build mode makes the same `__VC_PLATFORM__` seam available to an Electron shell, so a desktop session that bound a port would resolve to `ios`. No desktop shell binds it today: the only desktop code in the private repo is the S0 feasibility harness, which runs with no preload and therefore no injection path. Nothing is reported either way, since `telemetryHostAllowed` returns false for every wrapped mode before it reads the hostname, so a wrapped build computes the dimension into its enrichment props and sends no event. Deliberately NOT pre-fixed (party ruling 2026-07-29): a flag-based label only works if the desktop shell injects a port, which its command-seam design has not settled, and the vocabulary belongs with the data map and consent design that story produces. The stale premise that iOS is the only wrapper was corrected in PRD F1, F2, and F8, arch sections 2, 3, and 9, and `src/platform/types.ts` so that story starts from an accurate contract. `/bmad-code-review`. |
| 2026-07-29 | desktop-menu-undo-scope | #722 | desktop-shell | design-decision | P3 | low | — | open | **Ready.** Deferred from the /gds-code-review of PR #715 (rounds 3 and 4). `hostCommands.ts` routes `undo`/`redo` straight to `cb.onUndo`/`onRedo` with no equivalent of the `ownsNativeUndo` yield `inputKeys.ts:56` implements, so the menu Edit > Undo with the tower-rename field focused rolls back a TOWER action rather than the text edit. The module documents this as deliberate (a menu item is a global affordance, a keystroke is field-local, and the toolbar arrow is tower-scoped too), and that reasoning stands on its own. The problem is that `menu.ts`, the shell README, and the story Hazard 2 all justify the whole no-accelerator design by citing protection of that exact field, so clicking the item does what those docs call unacceptable. Decide one: (a) menu undo stays tower-scoped and the no-accelerator rationale stops citing the rename field, since the accelerator-collision argument holds without it; or (b) the field needs protecting from any source, and `dispatch` grows the `ownsNativeUndo` check. Also in scope: `undo`/`redo` currently run during a live editor press because the editor-busy guard only refuses `OPENS_A_DIALOG`, and an undo can mutate the facility the press is acting on, which is arguably worse than the stats refresh the frame loop already declines. Needs a product decision, not a fix. `/gds-code-review`. |
| 2026-07-29 | desktop-emoji-icons | — | accessibility | bug | P3 | low | — | resolved | **RESOLVED 2026-08-02 (PR #733).** Every functional UI and bulletin emoji is now an inline pixel icon (`src/ui/icons.ts`): 24x24 currentColor paths, most generated from the pinned `pixelarticons` dependency and inlined at build (`iconPaths.generated.ts`), the rest hand-drawn originals; the bulldoze tool is a two-tone red/amber wrecking ball. The engine still emits emoji as text and only the render layer swaps the leading marker, so `src/engine` stays DOM-free. `iconCoverage.guard.test.ts` fails if the engine emits an unmapped bulletin emoji or an icon bakes an off-palette fill. `#721` closes on merge via the PR trailer. Original: Functional UI icons are Unicode emoji rendered as text, not image assets (`src/index.html:131` is `<button id="audio-toggle">🔊</button>`; same for 💾 save, 📊 stats, ⚙ settings, 🗺 map, 🏙 city, and the in-game 💣 🚗 🚒 🔥 💰 🏆 set). On a system with no color-emoji font the supplementary-plane glyphs tofu while BMP symbols (★ ✕ ☰ ⚙ ↻) still draw, so the toolbar looks half-broken with NO error: nothing 404s, the console is clean, and the smoke passes. Confirmed during Linux verification 2026-07-29 (`fc-list`: 20 fonts, zero emoji), and specifically ruled out as an asset problem (case audit: 58 files, 1036 refs, 0 casing bugs; zero console errors). Mainstream desktop distributions ship an emoji font, so most desktop Linux users are fine; the exposure is bare or minimal distros, containers, and WSL, and a PACKAGED app cannot assume a host font the way a browser tab effectively can. Fix shape: inline SVG for the functional icons rather than bundling Noto Color Emoji (~10MB for a handful of glyphs), which also fixes the cross-platform inconsistency where the same emoji looks materially different on Windows, macOS, and Android. Decorative emoji in copy can stay as text. Player-facing (version bump + screenshot regeneration in the pinned container). Not caused by the command seam; #715 only made it visible by putting the game in a packaged app on a bare system. `/bmad-code-review`. |
| 2026-07-29 | help-dialog-double-scrollbar | — | accessibility | bug | P2 | low | — | done | **RESOLVED (this PR; #717 closes on merge).** The outer scrollbar was the `#modal` dialog element itself: current Chromium's UA stylesheet gives an open `<dialog>` `overflow: auto` (verified with a bare dialog in Chromium 149: computed `overflow-y: auto` with zero page CSS), so once Help's content passed `.modal-box`'s 82vh cap the dialog element became a second live scroller next to the box's own, and it also clipped the box's `.win` drop shadow. No repo CSS added it, which is why it reproduced in both the shell and the browser with no code change. Fix: `#modal` now pins `overflow: visible`, so the box stays the dialog's single scroller; this covers Statistics, Saves, and Settings too, since every dialog shares the `#modal` > `.modal-box` shell (measured: none of them adds a scroller of its own). New guard `src/tests/modalSingleScroller.guard.test.ts` pins the rule from the CSS source: `#modal` declares `overflow: visible`, nothing may re-scroll it, the dialog subtree's base cascade scrolls exactly one element (`.modal-box`), and media blocks may re-scroll only the ratified `.es-grid` well (#513). Mutation-checked red on the pre-fix CSS and on an injected second scroller. Original: Owner-reported while driving the packaged desktop shell 2026-07-29: the Help dialog shows TWO vertical scrollbars, an outer one on the dialog and an inner one on the guide body. This is a regression of the same class as #513 (the schedule dialog's double scrollbar, fixed in `040b53da`, deferral closed in `d6c44928`), so the earlier fix did not generalize to the Help dialog and nothing guards the pattern. Reproduces in the browser as well as the shell, so it is not desktop-specific and the desktop seam did not cause it. Fix shape: one scroll container per dialog. Find which of the dialog shell and the guide body owns the overflow, give the other `overflow: visible` / `min-height: 0`, and add a regression test asserting exactly one scrollable descendant, since a per-dialog manual fix is what let this come back. Check the other long dialogs (Statistics, Saves, Settings) in the same pass. Player-facing (version bump + screenshot regeneration in the pinned Playwright container). `/bmad-code-review`. |
| 2026-07-29 | desktop-help-page-link | #720 | desktop-shell | bug | P2 | low | — | open | **Ready.** The Help dialog's "Open the full help page" link is silently dead in the Electron shell: no window, no navigation, no console error, and nothing in the shell's refusal record. Two causes stacked: it is `target="_blank"` in a shell whose `setWindowOpenHandler` denies every open (the correct posture), and it is not passed through `routeExternalInWrapper` (that is applied only to `.help-report a` and the crash screen report link); and `/help` is an extensionless route, so even with a window it would resolve to `dist/help` and 404, because the clean URL only works where a host rewrites it. A dist scan found exactly three such references: `/help`, `/gallery`, and `/` (the last already resolves, since the protocol handler maps an empty path to `index.html`). Secondary finding worth keeping: the click was blocked WITHOUT producing a `windows` refusal entry, so the smoke's "zero blocked window opens" assertion is weaker than it reads. Proper fix is #718 (Help and Gallery in a real secondary window), which must add an extensionless-route rule to the resolver rather than two special cases. Cheap interim if #718 is not next: withhold the link in a wrapped build the way the install button already is, since a dead control is worse than no control and the dialog carries the same content. Not caused by the command seam (#715). `/bmad-code-review`. |
| 2026-07-29 | help-gallery-secondary-window | #718 | desktop-shell | feature-request | P3 | low | — | open | **Needs a spec.** Owner proposal 2026-07-29: open Help (and add Gallery) as a real secondary window in the desktop shell, so reading either does not cover the tower, plus make "Back to game" close that window rather than navigate. Findings that shape it: (a) `/help` and `/gallery` already exist as standalone prerendered pages (`dist/help.html`, `dist/gallery.html`), so no new content is needed, and Gallery has NO in-game dialog at all, only the page, so a Gallery menu item cannot route through the command seam the way the other nine commands do. (b) Navigating the MAIN window to either page would destroy the running sim, and the shell's `will-navigate` guard would permit it because both are same-origin, so a second window is the only safe shape. (c) `pageShell` renders "Back to game" as `<a href="/">`, which in a popup would boot a SECOND game instance inside the popup rather than returning to the first, so the page shell needs to learn it is in a secondary window and close instead. (d) PREREQUISITE: the shell's `will-navigate` and `setWindowOpenHandler` guards are attached to `mainWindow.webContents` only, so a second window would inherit none of them. A global `app.on("web-contents-created")` guard (plus `will-frame-navigate`, `will-redirect`, and explicit `webviewTag: false`) has to land first, and is worth doing regardless of this feature. (e) Keep the in-game Help dialog as the default on every platform: a second OS window is worse on a handheld and with a controller, and removing the dialog would fork the interface shared with web, Android, and iOS. So this is additive, desktop-only, and opt-in. Belongs to the private distribution repo's shell work plus a small public change for the page-shell close behavior. `/gds-code-review` for the public half. |

| 2026-07-29 | longpress-peek-dismiss-latch | — | mobile-touch-play | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | resolved | **FIXED in story 696-1 (v2.6.1); issue #696 closes on merge.** Party ruling: a long-press is explicit fresh intent like a tap, so `onLongPress` spends the ✕-dismissal latch through the same `resetLatch()` seam `selectPicked` uses (one intent gate, two callers); hover picks alone still respect the latch and pure touch is unaffected. Pinned by a wiring test and a controller-level seam test. ORIGINAL DEFER (kept for the record): **Deferred from the `/bmad-code-review` of the long-press-peek re-land (v2.6.0, PR #636 follow-up; Acceptance Auditor and Edge Case Hunter independently).** On a hybrid touch+mouse device, the hover card's coarse-tier ✕ sets the inspector's `inspectDismissed` latch (`src/game/inspector.ts`); a touch long-press on that same facility routes through `inspectPicked`, which early-returns on the latch, so the peek raises no card (GDD AC1 promises one) while `longPressFired` still swallows the release: the hold is silently eaten. Pure-touch devices unaffected (the peek card has no ✕ and `hide()` never arms the latch). Fix shape: decide whether a long-press is an explicit re-request like a tap (then spend the latch, mirroring `selectPicked` -> `resetLatch()`) or a glance like a hover (then at least skip the swallow when `inspectPicked` declines to show); the first reads more honest. Touch input path only, no engine or save impact. `/bmad-code-review`. |
| 2026-07-29 | congestion-gripe-binding-shaft | — | economy-satisfaction | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | resolved | **RESOLVED in the binding-shaft copy PR (v2.5.1, story 701-1; closes #701).** The congestion builder now emits per-floor per-class attribution beside the ratios (one shared build, ratios byte-identical), and `bindingTransportClassAt` names the BINDING shaft's kind with the party's tie rule: a walkway flips the wording only when strictly worse than every serving elevator beyond 1e-9 (ties are the default under the capacity-proportional split, so they keep the v2.4.1 elevator wording and can never ride float noise or build order). Mixed floors bound by a cross-loaded walkway get the new remedy line pointing at the floors only the walkway serves; the churn note reads the binding BEFORE the unit empties so the departing tenant's own load still counts. Facet 2 (segments) was dissolved by construction per the party ruling: the copy relays the model's own attribution, so segment honesty arrives if and when the model goes segment-aware, a model story with no copy work remaining. Originally: **Deferred from the `/gds-code-review` of the transport-aware congestion gripe copy (story 699-1, PR #700; Edge Case Hunter medium + Blind Hunter, merged).** `servingTransportKindsAt` answers "which passenger kinds stop at this floor" (skip lists honored, staff-only excluded), which closes the #699 skip-list seam, but not "which serving shaft binds": the v2 model sets a floor's congestion to its WORST serving shaft with load accumulated across every floor that shaft serves. Facet 1 (kind priority): an elevator-served floor whose binding shaft is a cross-loaded stair link still reads "crowded elevators. Add cars", a partially wrong lever. Facet 2 (segments): on a gap-split floor the classifier counts a shaft the tenant's segment cannot reach; consistent with the segment-blind congestion measure but contradicts what the tenant can board (#647 sphere). Refinement: name the binding shaft's kind, which needs `spatialCongestionByFloor` to expose per-shaft attribution (`loadByShaft` is internal today), and a ruling on whether copy may outrun the floor-global model on split floors. Scope-capped out of #700 by the design party (2026-07-29); v1's scalar noun looseness accepted as no worse than the pre-#700 copy. Fold-in candidate whenever the congestion model goes segment-aware. `/gds-code-review`. |
| 2026-07-29 | attendance-coverage-hole | #705 | economy-satisfaction | design-decision | P1 | med | — | idea | **Filed from the #548 demand-calibration design party (2026-07-29); the calibration makes this reachable, so it graduates from a footnote to a row.** Coverage for the unmet-local-demand drain reads only the RETAIL pool: `reachableVenuesByOrigin` and `retailVenueCount` exclude attendance venues (cinema, party hall) by design (#424). Design question: should attendance capacity count toward coverage, and how, given that widening the coverage denominator without widening the income pool breaks the share identity, and the 1994 evaluation read commerce. Options: a separate attendance-coverage term in `unmetCoverage`, an attendance-aware exemption tier, or ruling that entertainment does not feed residents and the copy says so. Chained to the same telemetry gate as the #548 perCapita retune. `/gds-code-review`. |

| 2026-07-28 | sky-lobby-ghost-walkers | — | pixel-art | bug | P2 | med | — | resolved | **FIXED in the ghost-crowd PR (v1.104.0), closes #639.** Reported by the owner: a lobby built on floor 15+ with no way to reach it still filled with ambient walkers. Cause: `buildWalkers` spawns lobby-concourse figures with `perFloor: false`, and `updateMotion` gated those only on tower-wide busyness (`min(1, population/350) * night`), which says nothing about whether anyone can get to that floor. Corridor loiterers were never affected because `perFloor: true` gates them on their own floor's live occupancy. Fix: `updateMotion` now AND-gates non-`perFloor` figures on `engine.sim.floorReachable(w.floor)`. Reachability and not `isFloorServed`, because it runs the passenger router, so Classic's walkway-willingness budget correctly hides a served-but-stranded lobby (no commuter spawns for it either) while Modern's served-equals-reachable needs no era branch. Floor 1 short-circuits to true in the engine, so the main lobby is untouched. The verdict is already memoized engine-side per `tower.revision` in a per-sim WeakMap, so no render-side cache was added (and so nothing new had to be hand-invalidated on sim swap in `towerScene`). Stair and escalator climbers carry the same `perFloor: false` and are covered by the same gate. `src/render/excalibur/towerCrowd.ts`; the walker-construction path was split out to `towerWalkerBuild.ts` to keep both files under the size ceiling. `/gds-code-review`. |
| 2026-07-28 | landing-tile-router-parity | — | pixel-art | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | resolved | **RESOLVED in the parity-test PR (v1.104.1), closes #674.** Raised by the Blind Hunter on the stair-reachability PR and independently by the Edge Case Hunter. `landingTile` (render) is a second copy of the router's `landingSeg` rule; they agreed by construction but nothing pinned it, so a fix to `segment-shaft-multi-run` (#662) could have split them silently and undone #665 clause 2. `towerWalkerBuild.test.ts` now asserts `segAt(tower, floor, landingTile(tower, t, floor)) === landingSeg(tower, t, floor)` on real `Tower` fixtures. The case that earns the assertion is a footprint straddling TWO runs: within a single run every column resolves to the same segment, so picking a different tile there is not a divergence at all (verified by mutation, a reversed scan in `landingSeg` leaves the single-run cases green). The two-run fixture is exactly the #662 shape, and it does go red when the engine's rule moves. Also pinned: placement REFUSES a flight with no structural column under its span, so the `t.x` fallback both sides carry is defensive rather than a live path. **Rename follow-through (v1.104.2, #662):** #662 renamed `landingSeg` -> `landingSegs` (returns every overlapped run). The parity test moved with it: it now asserts `segAt(tower, floor, landingTile(...)) === landingSegs(tower, t, floor)[0]` (the leftmost run `landingTile` picks), still green. Full bipartite linking keeps `landingTile`'s first-run choice valid: all runs sharing a flight are one connected component, so the leftmost run is reachable exactly when the flight is usable. `/gds-code-review`. |
| 2026-07-28 | walker-reachability-refinements | — | pixel-art | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | resolved | **RESOLVED in the stair-reachability PR (v1.104.1), closes #665.** Deferred from the `/gds-code-review` of the ghost-crowd PR (v1.104.0); the segment-granularity half shipped there, and the two stair clauses shipped here. (1) The gate asked only about a flight's BOTTOM landing, so a stair rising to a floor no route ends at still showed figures trudging up toward it. A climber now carries both landings (`altFloor`/`altTileX` on `Walker`) and needs BOTH reachable. The probe already routes through stairs, so a reachable bottom normally implies a reachable top; the ends only disagree when Classic's walkway-willingness budget cuts the flight, and then nobody completes that climb from either side. Note the fix is an AND, not the either-endpoint OR the deferral text first suggested: an OR would have left the reported case (bottom reachable, top not) showing exactly the ghosts it was raised about. (2) A climber probed its transport's own tile `t.x`, but `validateTransport` only requires SOME tile under the footprint to be structural, so on a gap-split floor a stair may legally sit with its leftmost columns over the void; the probe then found no run and hid the climbers on a usable flight. `landingTile` now walks the footprint for the first structural tile, mirroring the router's `landingSeg` including its `t.x` fallback, so the two cannot disagree. `buildWalkers` turned out to be unit-testable after all (it bakes no `ex.Canvas`, it hands out the pre-baked `personGfx`), so the construction path gained real coverage in `towerWalkerBuild.test.ts` and its header no longer claims to be Playwright-only. `/gds-code-review`. |

| 2026-07-28 | walker-reachability-refinements | #665 | pixel-art | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | partial | **Deferred from the `/gds-code-review` of the ghost-crowd PR (v1.104.0). The segment half SHIPPED in that PR; the stair-endpoint half is what remains.** DONE: the gate now asks per POSITION, `sim.positionReachable(w.floor, w.tileX)`, so a gap-split Modern floor (#647 / PR #652) hides the stranded run's figures while a sibling run of the same floor keeps its own. OPEN: a stair or escalator climber carries its transport's BOTTOM floor (`spawnWalker(..., t.bottom, t.x, false)`), so a flight whose bottom is reachable but whose top is not still shows climbers. Reachable in Classic: with `WALKWAY_WILLINGNESS.stairs = 4`, six stacked stairways from floor 1 make floors 1-5 reachable and floor 6 not, and the 5-to-6 flight carries `floor = 5`. Fails safe (shows rather than hides). Fix: gate on either endpoint, which needs the transport's top floor carried on the `Walker` alongside `floor`. ALSO OPEN (same family, from the final confirming pass): a climber probes its transport's own tile `t.x`, but `validateTransport` only requires SOME tile under the 8-wide footprint to be structural, so on a gap-split floor a stair may legally sit with its leftmost column over the void. Routing links the flight by its landing tile while the render probe asks about `t.x`, finds no run there, and hides every climber on a perfectly usable stair. Fails safe (hides rather than ghosts) and is unreachable on a gap-free floor, which takes the single-segment fast path. Fix: probe the first structural tile under the footprint, mirroring `landingSeg`. `/gds-code-review`. |

| 2026-07-28 | saves-overwrite-unreadable-slot | — | title-screen-load-tower | review-deferral | P2 | med | — | resolved | **FIXED in the Load Tower PR (#655), closes #657.** Raised by the `/bmad-code-review` Blind Hunter and re-raised by Codex as a P1 once `exists` became strictly loadability-based, which broadened the set of rows that read as empty. `savesTemplate` now distinguishes present-but-unreadable from absent using the new `SlotInfo.present`: the row is labeled `Couldn't be read by this version.` and loses its **Save** button, since overwriting is the one action that destroys the bytes a later build may recover. **Delete** stays, because clearing a slot you have been told is unreadable is a deliberate choice rather than a silent loss. Two tests pin it. ORIGINAL DEFER (kept for the record): Data loss, and pre-existing: the in-game Saved Towers manager gates its per-row **Save** button on the parse-based `s.exists` (`src/ui/templates/saves.ts`), so a slot that is PRESENT but unreadable by this build renders as "empty" with a Save button. Saving over it destroys bytes that a later build may well have recovered, with no confirmation, which is exactly what `SaveGame.preserveUnreadable` exists to prevent for the autosave. The title screen's new picker labels that same slot "Couldn't be read by this version." and offers no control, so the two surfaces now describe the same slot differently. The fix is available: `SlotInfo.present` (added in that PR) distinguishes absent from unreadable, so the manager can label the row and either drop Save or route it through a confirm. Not fixed in the Load Tower PR because it is the in-game manager's behavior, untouched by that change. `/bmad-code-review`. |

Driven by zoom (rides the bed distance filter), live occupancy (`ViewFocus.crowd`, honest-rooms: empty venues stay silent), and the sim clock (`ViewFocus.hour`: offices workday-gated, condos midday-quiet). Audition rules enforced in code and pinned by tests: voices shift down only, every noise source behind a steep rolloff -48 filter at warm cutoffs (a graph regression test walks every noise node). Earlier interim step v1.61.1 quieted the old accents; this removes them (`maybeAccent`/`accentHit`/`scheduleStep` retired, `toneVoices` is jingles only). `/gds-code-review` ran (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor against the GDD): the triage patched every mechanics deviation back to spec (silence threshold, honest talker formula, per-scene murmur gains and fixed voices, element-rate scaling, cluster math, program start/stop on live attendance, scene-key settling, the 1 Hz census cache, slot and timer lifecycle races, buffer-source reaping, granular program teardown) plus the owner's two live reports (phone crackle: playback latency hint + capped polyphony + soft dark one-shot envelopes; flat-feeling volume slider: perceptual squared curve); five findings recorded in the Deferral inbox (three texture defers, one evidence note, one dismissal with evidence). **Historical context (the pre-delivery proposal this row was created from; delivered above):** the tower looked alive but did not sound alive: `src/audio/` has a generic per-scene "room rush" noise bed plus a few sparingly-fired zoomed-in accents (`ding`/`clatter`/`keys`/`rumble`/`boom`/`register`/`chatter`), which read as texture, not people. Deepen (not rewrite) that layer into a real crowd bed keyed to what the player views and to live census (honest-rooms pillar: a fuller venue sounds fuller): talking/murmur that scales with crowd, walking/footfall on lobby+transit, eating (cutlery+chatter) for food, playing for recreation, a muffled movie-soundtrack murmur for cinema, and party-hall ambience (music-through-a-wall + cheer) for weddings/parties. Felt zoomed out, resolves to per-venue detail on zoom-in (reuse the existing `detail` ramp + `bedFilter`). All procedural (no files), through the music/ambience volume bus + mute, gesture-gated. Player-facing, so it shipped with a version bump and changelog note (v1.62.0). Landed as its own PR alongside the #479/#480 music work; `/gds-code-review` ran as recorded above. |

The day split made the conflation load-bearing per day, and "unmeasured" is derived by duplicate positive-scans (`warm()` in the dialog, `measuredDay()` in the engine), a drift trap for future consumers. Fix: first-class sampled state on `HourlyByDay` (per-ring sampled count or bitmask, still transient), warm a sampled-but-zero day, draw zero dashes, collapse the duplicated scans. Rendering an inline markdown subset in the modal was considered and deferred: the update prompt is the reload-critical surface, so any markdown-to-HTML there opens an XSS lane for questionable benefit on 1 to 3 short lines. | 2026-07-16 | deserialize-coercion | — | save-hardening | review-deferral | P2 | med | — | done | **IMPLEMENTED (issue #306); issue closes on merge.** `Simulation.deserialize` assigned the top-level `money` and clock `minutes` straight from the parsed save, skipping the finite/range coercion every other field gets, so a forged or corrupt save (`money: NaN`, `minutes: NaN`) poisoned the ledger, rating gates, and every `clock.minutes` consumer (completeAt/vacate timers, the lunch bulletin) with NaN. Now money is coerced to a finite number (falling back to the constructor starting balance, sign preserved so legal debt survives) and minutes to a non-negative finite number before the `Clock` is built. Minutes is deliberately NOT floored: real play accumulates sub-minute steps, so a valid fractional save round-trips exactly (idempotence preserved, golden master unchanged). The `star` field was already hardened by a prior change; this completes the set. `/gds-code-review` ran (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor); the one patch fixed a fractional-minutes idempotence regression the first cut introduced (dropped the `Math.floor`) and strengthened the tests to pin the fallback values; two magnitude-cap defers recorded in the inbox. Guarded by `storage.integration.test.ts` (forged NaN/Infinity/negative/string money, star, and minutes, plus a fractional round-trip and legal-debt round-trip). |

| 2026-07-16 | weather-shapes-crowd | — | SimTower parity | feature-request | P3 | — | — | done | **DONE (v1.52.0, PR for #430).** Rain now thins the drawn crowd at the spawn layer via a new `GameRules.rainCrowdFactor()` (Classic 0.5 matching the canon shopper hit, Modern 0.7 softer), applied to the `spawnStep` accumulator in `src/engine/crowd/spawn.ts` alongside the existing time-of-day and population factors. It draws no RNG (the accumulator, not the seeded stream), so a clear or cloudy day multiplies by exactly 1 and stays byte-identical; both golden-master hashes are unchanged. A thinner crowd is what drops an attendance venue's live fill (`customersIn`), so a rainy cinema now earns less because fewer people show up; to end the double-count now that attendance income reads the live fill (#424), `rainMult` in `collectTrafficIncome` is dropped for attendance venues and kept for retail (retail income is statistical via the demand pool, not crowd-read, so the drawn crowd is not its channel; `rainMult` stays retail's one rain source). The crowd reads the authoritative `sim.weather` the loop passes in (single source shared with the income loop, so the two layers cannot disagree within a tick), falling back to the pure `weatherFor(clock.day)` hash for crowd-only paths. `/gds-code-review` ran (3 adversarial layers): one patch (single-source the crowd/economy weather read, which had skewed by one hour at each day boundary; both reviewers flagged it), the retail-double-penalty flag dismissed with evidence (retail income is statistical; `crowd.rng` is a separate RNG from `sim.rng`; golden master byte-identical), four defers recorded below. Guarded by `weatherCrowd.integration.test.ts` (crowd thins on rain; reads the passed weather not the day-hash; retail keeps rainMult at 0.5x0.6, attendance no longer pays it; a cinema fills less over a rainy afternoon end to end) and the `gameRules.test.ts` 0.5/0.7 pins. Player note in the changelog. Original context: weather affected commerce only through the `rainMult` multiplier while the crowd was weather-blind, so the visible crowd stayed full in the rain while money quietly dropped. `/gds-code-review`. |

| 2026-07-15 | graduated-lobby-distance-eval | — | SimTower parity | feature-request | P2 | med | — | done | **IMPLEMENTED v1.44.0 (issue #394); issue closes on merge.** A graduated satisfaction pressure keyed on floors from the nearest (sky)lobby, applied to office/condo/hotel, folded into the single per-tick placement-erosion step with a new `lobbyFar` vacate cause. FAR caps satisfaction (a ceiling, never evicts); VERY FAR (past the lobby-ladder reach) also erodes gently and can evict, so a skipped sky lobby costs tenants while building them every 15 floors keeps every floor capped-at-worst. The Classic/Modern split rides `GameRules.lobbyDistanceDrain` (Classic two discrete bands; Modern a smoother continuous curve, live distance in the inspector). | 2026-07-15 | leave-tower-unmet-demand | — | SimTower parity | feature-request | P2 | med | — | done | **DONE (v1.50.0, PR #437 for #395). From `gdd-simtower-optimization-gaps-2026-07-15`.** A served office/condo/hotel whose reachable retail coverage is below a floor is capped (both modes) and, in Modern, eroded toward a notice with a new `unmetDemand` vacate cause. Coverage = `min(1, 1/share)` from the statistical demand map (`computeDemandMap`, no RNG); the drain folds into the SINGLE shared placement-erosion step in `satisfaction.ts` (steepest cap, max erosion) and diverges only through `GameRules.unmetDemandDrain` (Classic cap-only; Modern cap + erosion past the evict floor). A tower with NO retail BUILT is exempt (an office building with no shops is the baseline, not a problem), keyed on the count of built operational retail venues (`retailVenueCount`, reachable or not); a tower that HAS retail penalizes a tenant that can reach none (coverage 0), including when all of its retail sits on stranded floors. Attendance venues (cinema/party hall) do not count as retail coverage (resolves the open decision on the `attendance-venue-demand` deferral note below). Extracted `dominantGripe`/`vacateCause`/`unmetCoverage` into `src/engine/sim/gripe.ts` to stay under the file-size guard. Golden master unchanged (fixture is fully covered). `/gds-code-review` ran (3 layers); one patch fixed (the no-retail exemption was keyed on `share`, wrongly exempting a stranded tenant in a retail-having tower) and defers recorded below. Guarded by `gameRules.test.ts`, `unmetDemand.integration.test.ts`, and the `unmetCoverage`/`dominantGripe` cases. |
| 2026-07-15 | contiguous-skylobby-transfer | — | SimTower parity | feature-request | P3 | — | — | done | **DONE (v1.51.0, PR #441 for #396).** Classic now admits a passenger transfer involving an express elevator (express to or from standard/stairs/escalator) only at a (sky) lobby floor, ground floor 1 included, via the new `GameRules.expressTransferNeedsLobby` seam (Classic true, Modern false: Modern keeps the forgiving any-shared-stop routing, byte-identical BFS path). The gate lives in the crowd routing BFS (`src/engine/crowd/routing.ts`): edges carry the shaft's express flag and Classic runs a state-per-arrival-class search so a gated express arrival can never shadow a legal all-local route (the strand risk). v1 is the FLOOR-LEVEL rule (lobby tile anywhere on the floor admits the transfer); tile-level contiguous-lobby-span contiguity between the two shafts is documented in-code as a deliberate later refinement. MAX_RIDES stays 2; golden-master hashes unchanged in both modes. `/gds-code-review` ran (3 layers, no high/medium code defect): patched the stale "3+ rides" player copy (stats legend, daily nudge, inspector access line) to the accurate "no route from the lobby within two rides", tightened the seam docs (express-to-express is gated too), and added stairs-to-express, express-to-express, and no-express-parity test pins; three defers recorded in the Deferral inbox (pre-1.48 save retroactivity advisory, dispatch serving gate-stranded floors, tile-level lobby contiguity). Validated against a real 100-floor player save (sky lobbies every 15 floors): zero floors strand. Guarded by `skylobbyTransfer.integration.test.ts` and `gameRules.test.ts`. Original context: In 1994 a sim transfers express to standard only through a contiguous sky lobby touching both; our routing allowed a transfer at any shared stop via implicit graph adjacency. | 2026-07-21 | venue-crowd-real-occupancy | — | pixel-art | feature-request | P3 | — | — | resolved | **RESOLVED in the ghost-crowd PR (v1.104.0), closes #552.** Folded from the deferral inbox by #545 (E3/E5/E6 pixel-art reviews plus the `venue-people-routing` traffic-seam defers). Clause by clause: (1) the metro-platform and party-hall `scatterPeople` calls were retired in E5/E3, and this PR deleted the now-callerless helper itself from `sprites/common.ts`, so the idiom cannot come back by accident. (2) The engine-side visible-attendance count the row asked for had ALREADY shipped with #424's attendance-visit flow (`Facility.attendance`, `Unit.customersIn`, `syncAttendanceOccupants` mirroring into `occupants`, census-inert because catalog `population` stays 0, and already a bake-signature input): cinema, party hall and aquatic center draw a real house. This PR closed the last hole in that contract, `drawWeddingHall`, which took no `Unit` at all and so painted the couple plus two full rows of guests into every hall including an untenanted one. It now fills to `visibleOccupants(u)`, couple first. Party hall and aquatic center moved from raw `u.occupants` to `visibleOccupants(u)` to match the spec's Always-rule (behavior-neutral: `outForMeal` is only ever written on a meal trip's ORIGIN room, never on an attendance venue). The unsubtyped shop's `hash(u.id) > 0.4` fallback, which painted a permanent shopper into roughly three of every five empty shops, is gone too. Entrance staff are gated on `tower.units.some(isPresent)`, the party having ratified GATE: a lease keeps `state` at `occupied` around the clock, so a retail-only tower keeps its doorman between meal windows (a live-body count like `totalPopulation` would not). A hotel-only tower does go unstaffed between the housekeeping sweep and the evening check-ins, which follows the leases. (3) The baked ~17px stair/escalator incline rider (AC 151) is RETIRED, not deferred: a baked rider on `cache:true` flight art would show a phantom climber on empty stairs, which contradicts the very empty-tower invariant this row exists to serve. Routed climbers already ride over the flight. `/gds-code-review`. |

| 2026-07-15 | stairs-willingness-canon | — | SimTower parity | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | done | **CLASSIC ENFORCEMENT SHIPPED 2026-07-19 (v1.65.0, PR #501; #384 closed on merge). Follow-ups #502 (Modern penalty) and #503 (MAX_RIDES) carry the remaining work on their own rows.** The party's re-scope was implemented as spec'd: `bfsRouteExpressGated` (Classic passengers) rewritten from a level-BFS to a fewest-edges BFS with state `(floor, arrived-by-express, rides, walkRun, runCap)`. Stair/escalator edges (tagged `walkKind` in `buildAdjacency`) spend a SEPARATE contiguous-walk budget (stairs 4, escalators 7; stricter governs a mixed run) that resets to zero on boarding any car, so pure-stair reach LOOSENS from 2 to 4 as canon requires; the ride budget stays `MAX_RIDES = 2` and the #396 express-transfer gate still applies to a walk off an express arrival. `bfsRoute` (Modern + all staff routing) untouched. Design authored via `gds-gdd` (parity GDD §8) + spec; `/gds-code-review` clean (composite-key optimality, 4/7 boundary, determinism, O(E) bounded-constant, applied fail-closed guard + tightened types). TDT parity: willingness is live routing, NOT persisted; export byte-identical (404532 B) and the Wine carryover renders unchanged. TWO FOLLOW-UPS spun out (both owner-gated, do NOT ship autonomously): (a) **Modern comfort-penalty** at the same knee (climb allowed, tenant unhappy, eventually leaves) as a separate satisfaction-model track (GDD epic E6, own Samus tuning pass) - see row `modern-walkway-comfort-penalty`; (b) **`MAX_RIDES = 2` may be too small** for tall multi-transfer elevator journeys (express -> sky-lobby-standard -> local), a possible SECOND bug independent of stairs (GDD open question 11.4) - see row `max-rides-multitransfer-budget`. OUTCOME: PR #501 merged (v1.65.0); the follow-up rows #502 and #503 carry the remainder. Prior ruling retained below. **CANON RATIFIED 2026-07-19 (web research + design party): STAIRS = 4, ESCALATORS = 7 contiguous flights per trip (an elevator ride resets the count).** Sources converge (roadwolf "4 sets of stairs per trip"; relentlessoptimizer "maximum of four flights"; GameFAQs); the lone "5 levels" counts the origin floor. SHIPPED as canon: `WALKWAY_WILLINGNESS` in `src/engine/facilityCaps.ts` + a canon test + PARITY.md note (the constant landed in PR #499; Classic enforcement then shipped in PR #501). **PARTY RULING on enforcement (owner greenlight required, does NOT ship autonomously):** it splits by mode. **Classic** = a HARD refusal past the threshold (the 1994 wall) counting contiguous walk legs with an elevator resetting the count; small scope (one router branch + the constant + a test). **Modern** = a SOFT satisfaction/comfort penalty at the SAME threshold (climb allowed, tenant unhappy, pays less, eventually leaves) rather than a refusal, because uncapped stairs are a dominant strategy that deletes the elevator game; larger scope (a new term in the tenant-satisfaction model, its own GDD + Samus tuning pass) and MUST NOT block the Classic cap. Both rulesets read the one `WALKWAY_WILLINGNESS` constant. **CORRECTION 2026-07-19 (verified by probing `bfsRoute` directly): the earlier "engine has NO cap" premise is WRONG.** The router caps a whole trip at `MAX_RIDES` = 2 boardings, and each single stair flight is one boarding, so pure-stair reach is currently **2 floors** (a 3-flight climb is REFUSED today), STRICTER than the canon 4, and stairs share that budget with elevators (no separate walk budget; a stair after an express->standard journey is already refused). So matching canon is NOT "add a cap" (more restrictive) but "give walk legs their OWN contiguous budget of 4/7 separate from the 2-ride elevator budget, reset by an elevator ride" (LOOSENS stair reach). That is a bigger BFS restructuring than the party scoped ("one router branch"), and it FLIPS the design premise the party + owner greenlight were based on: the owner should re-decide with these correct facts before implementation. OUTCOME: the Classic cap shipped (spec + `/gds-code-review` + v1.65.0, PR #501); the Modern comfort-penalty GDD is deferred to #502. Original: **RE-SCOPED 2026-07-19 (harness session): this is NOT a quick "pin an existing constant".** Two findings: (1) our engine does NOT model a cumulative stair-climb willingness cap at all - `src/engine/crowd/routing.ts` treats stairs/escalators as free walkable graph links with no floor-count limit (consistent with PARITY.md's "our staff routing has no such degradation"). So adding the cap is a genuine GAMEPLAY change (Samus feel-check + `/gds-code-review` + version bump), not a constant tweak. (2) The 4-vs-5 canon value can't be settled cheaply on the headless harness: it needs a behavioral study (build a stairs-only reach, run the sim, read tenant stress over time), not a single screenshot; the on-disc manual would state it but is game IP we keep out of git. NEXT: decide whether Modern/Classic should even model stair willingness before RE'ing the exact number; if yes, a dedicated behavioral harness run or an owner-ratified canon value. Original: **Gated: canon verification via the Wine harness. From gdd-simtower-parity-2026-07-06.** Sources conflict on how many cumulative floors a person will climb by stairs (4 vs 5). Verify in the real game and pin the constant; same species as walkway-variants. |

(1) The wait-based signal (#514's crowd EMA) is cadence-dependent, so feeding it into persisted satisfaction breaks headless/browser determinism (proven: a Modern tower's office satisfactions diverged across tick rates, `[…0.98,0.87]` coarse vs `[…0.66,0.59]` fine; Classic identical). So the crowd accumulator must stay read-only. (2) The only deterministic substitute, the forced stair-climb length to a floor, is redundant with the existing `lobbyFar` drain (a long forced climb IS being far from a lobby) and elevator-WAIT is already the `congestion` drain's job; in testing the penalty always resolved to the sharper `lobbyFar` gripe. The behavioral refinement (make the lobby-distance drain's advice correct on a stair-only floor: "run an elevator here", not "add a sky lobby") moves to **#436**. Spec: `spec-modern-commute-comfort.md`. Prior rulings retained below. Absorbs the deferred #384-Modern walkway penalty and the held-back Classic/Modern comparison line (compare.ts). Modern's track: accumulate a per-tenant commute discomfort from the tenant's actual wait/travel time (reusing the per-person `wait` already computed), surface it ("this tenant's commute is rough"), and feed a GENTLE satisfaction nudge. Also absorbs the deferred #384 Modern walkway penalty (one "commute/vertical hassle" term feeding both stair flights and elevator wait) and closes the per-Sim-stress gap #514 for Modern legibly. HARD GUARDRAIL: never a reachability wall (grumble + slow attrition only, never "won't move in", or it is `MAX_RIDES` reborn). Owner-tuned curve. Modern was left out of scope. Party ruling / GDD epic E6: Modern applies a comfort/satisfaction penalty at the SAME knee (climb allowed, tenant unhappy, pays less, eventually leaves) rather than a hard refusal, because uncapped stairs are a dominant strategy that deletes the elevator game. Owner-gated, does NOT ship autonomously: a new term in the tenant-satisfaction model + its own Samus tuning pass (own GDD track, not a router branch).
